{"parentUuid":"16048eb3-2a18-4780-bcc8-b3c254ecec60","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a60b3a901f88efa91","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- artifact-planner: Produce a self-contained, navigable HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPLIT STORYBOARD: each scene pairs a rich UI/mockup (left) with a before→after Mermaid diagram of the structural change (right), plus a \"what this lets you do\" line and an \"under the hood\" strip. Embeds a machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, walk through, or visualize any multi-step flow: onboarding, checkout, auth, OAuth integrations, wizards, subscription cancellation, push notifications, support ticket lifecycles, AI chat execution, CI/CD pipelines, refund flows, admin features, migrations, webhook lifecycles, or anything else with sequenced stages and decision points. Trigger on \"create an artifact for…\", \"plan this flow\", \"make a flow plan\", \"visualize this flow\", \"show me the stages of…\", \"mock up the flow for…\", \"I want to plan X visually\", \"build a walkthrough of…\", or any time the user has just described a feature/flow and would benefit from a single-page visual + structured spec they can revisit, share, or drop back into a new chat. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- artifact-planner-canvas: Produce a self-contained HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPATIAL CANVAS: the WHOLE plan on one pannable/zoomable Figma-style board — frontend mockups in a top band, each stage's before→after Mermaid diagram directly beneath (dashed spine connectors), branches as tinted lanes with fork/rejoin curves, decisions and open questions as sticky notes placed next to the stages they affect, and double-click-anywhere comment stickies. This is the CANVAS variant of `artifact-planner` (which pages one scene at a time as a split storyboard): prefer the canvas when the user wants to see everything at once, compare stages spatially, or leave free-floating comments; prefer the paged storyboard for linear walkthroughs and presentations. Embeds the same machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, or visualize any multi-step flow (onboarding, checkout, auth, wizards, CI/CD pipelines, migrations, webhook lifecycles…) AND signals a canvas: \"canvas artifact\", \"spatial plan\", \"put the plan on a canvas/board\", \"Figma-style board\", \"show me the whole flow at once\", \"map it out spatially\" — or when they ask for the canvas version of an existing artifact-plan. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- auto-rough-cut: Turn a folder of raw class-video recordings — separately recorded pristine voice tracks (Audacity .wav) plus their screen/camera videos (.mp4) — into a draggable Adobe Premiere Pro FCP7 XML that is silence-cut to Ray's Recut preferences, has the pristine audio synced and linked to the video, transcribes every kept chunk, and flags repeated takes / filler / breaths in PURPLE for a fast review pass. Use this skill whenever Ray points at a folder of recording pairs and wants a rough cut, first assembly, or \"silence cut\", or says things like \"cut the silences\", \"sync my audio and video and remove the dead air\", \"make a Premiere XML from these recordings\", \"apply my Recut process / record preferences\", \"flag the retakes\", \"auto rough-cut this video\", \"turn my Audacity audio + screen recording into a timeline\", or stages new recordings under a \"[Class Videos]\" / to-film folder. Trigger it even if Recut, transcription, or retakes aren't all named — any \"remove the silence and give me a Premiere XML I can still drag around\" request for paired audio+video is this skill. Distinct from recut-xml-fixer (which only repairs an XML Recut already exported); this skill does the whole cut from raw media.\n- auto-spec: Turn a feature brief (inline text or a stub plan file) into an implementation-ready plan via an autonomous two-agent interview, an external-API research verification pass, and a two-agent codebase-grounded planning pass — and, in its bootstrap mode, build/refresh the personalized digital twin that powers that interview by mining your Claude Code conversation history. Use when the user says \"auto spec\", \"spec autopilot\", \"auto interview\", \"flesh out this plan\", \"/auto-spec <file or brief>\", or otherwise wants a plan written without manually answering questions; ALSO use for \"create my auto-spec\", \"clone my brain\", \"make a spec twin\", \"personalize auto-spec\", \"build my decision profile\", or any request to analyze conversation history for decision patterns. Produces a single plan file with Feature Brief, Spec Decisions, External Research, Codebase Map, and phased Implementation Plan — ready to hand to `/implement-task` — or, in bootstrap mode, a refreshed `decision_profile.md` + `qa_examples.json` digital twin.\n- chronicle\n- codex-consult: OpenAI Codex CLI wrapper — three modes. Code review: independent diff review via\ncodex review with pass/fail gate. Challenge: adversarial mode that tries to break\nyour code. Consult: ask codex anything with session continuity for follow-ups.\nThe \"200 IQ autistic developer\" second opinion. Use when asked to \"codex review\",\n\"codex challenge\", \"ask codex\", \"second opinion\", or \"consult codex\".\n- improve-claude-md: improve a CLAUDE.md file using <important if> blocks to improve instruction adherence\n- interview: Relentlessly interrogate a plan, decision, or idea one question at a time until the user and Claude reach a genuinely shared understanding. Use this whenever the user wants to stress-test their thinking, pressure-test a plan, be challenged on an idea, or says any of \"interview me\", \"grill me\", \"grill this\", \"poke holes in this\", \"stress-test this\", \"ask me everything\", \"interrogate me\", \"what am I missing\", or \"/interview\". Also reach for it when the user drops a half-formed plan and asks for questions before you build anything, or when a request is underspecified enough that guessing would waste real work. Do not write code, files, or plans while this skill is active until the user says the picture is complete.\n- slackbot-message: Send Slack messages to a named channel via a bot user. Use this skill whenever you need to notify Ray on Slack, post a status update, alert a channel, deliver a short summary, or when he asks you to \"post to slack\", \"message #channel\", \"send a slack message\", \"ping slack\", \"slack me\", \"let me know on slack\", or names a specific Slack channel by name. Also use it after long-running tasks finish if Ray asked for Slack notifications. The bot is invited to the relevant channels, so the script can post to any channel by its human-readable name (no need to know channel IDs).\n- spec-developer-v2: Interview the user about a rough idea or brief with sharp, non-obvious product and technical questions, then write a concise implementation-ready spec. Use when the user wants to spec something out, develop a vague idea into requirements, or turn a product concept into a detailed plan.\n- sync-skills: Commit and push GLOBAL skill changes to the personal skills git repo\n(github.com/ray-amjad/private-skills). Auto-detects what changed in\n~/.claude/skills/, stages the changes, generates a meaningful commit message,\nand pushes to origin main. Use this skill whenever you finish creating or\nediting a global skill, or when the user says \"sync skills\", \"push skills\",\n\"save skills\", \"/sync-skills\", or asks to commit skill changes to git.\nIMPORTANT: Only trigger this skill when working at the project level (i.e., when\nthe working directory is a project repo, not from within ~/.claude/skills/ itself).\nDo NOT use this for project-scoped skills (e.g. skills under a project's own\n.claude/skills/, like ray-os's sentence-mining) — those are committed to that\nproject's repo, not here.\n- ultragoal\n- vercel-logs: Check Vercel production logs, errors, and build output. Use this skill whenever you need to\ndiagnose a deployment error, check for runtime errors, investigate 500s, or the user asks\n\"check the logs\", \"what's the error\", \"why is it broken\", \"check vercel logs\", or any\nrequest to look at Vercel deployment/runtime/build logs. Also trigger on `/vercel-logs`.\n\nCRITICAL: Also invoke this skill BEFORE running any `vercel logs` command yourself —\neven when no one asked you to. The CLI has non-obvious flag interactions (implicit\nfollow mode, stderr-only output, --since rejection) that cause hangs or silent failures\nif you guess at flags. If you're about to type `vercel logs` in a Bash call, load this\nskill first.\n- vercel-monitor: Watch a Vercel deployment in the background and notify when it reaches Ready/Error/Canceled.\nUse this skill whenever the user pushes to main, runs `git push`, deploys to Vercel, or asks\nto \"monitor the deploy\", \"watch the build\", \"check if it deployed\", or anything similar —\neven if they don't explicitly say \"Vercel\". Also trigger on `/vercel-monitor`. This replaces\nolder patterns of spawning a haiku subagent or polling via the Vercel MCP, both of which\nwaste tokens and context on large projects.\n- anki-cleanup\n- binary-explorer: Reverse-engineer any desktop application OR Claude Code's own binary to understand how it works behind the scenes — the APIs it calls, the internal flow, the models/providers it uses, its feature flags, config constants, embedded prompts, IPC protocols. For general apps it works on Electron apps (Wispr Flow, Slack, Discord, VS Code, Cursor, Notion, Linear…), native macOS .app bundles, single-executable bundles (Bun/Node/Deno/Go SEA), and raw Mach-O / ELF / PE binaries. It ALSO reverse-engineers Claude Code's own binary — it knows Claude Code's mangled variable conventions, feature-flag patterns, and embedded-prompt layout. Use this skill whenever the user points at a desktop app and wants to know \"how does X work under the hood\", \"what API does it call\", \"how does it do Y\", \"what's the flow for Z\", \"reverse engineer this app\", \"figure out how this feature works\", \"what endpoints does it hit\", \"does it run locally or in the cloud\", \"what model does it use\", or names a specific app path (.app, .exe, a binary). ALSO use it whenever the user asks about a Claude Code feature, feature flag, hidden toggle, internal prompt, or wants to understand how something in Claude Code works under the hood — especially unreleased, gated, or undocumented functionality — or says \"how does X work in the binary\", \"what does this feature flag do\", \"find the prompt for X\", \"reverse engineer X\", or mentions a specific codename/flag. Also trigger when the user wants to audit an app's network behavior, understand its architecture, or ext…\n- class-scriptwriter: Turn a raw idea, source tweet, video transcript, feature announcement, or rough outline into a polished video script for Ray's Agentic Coding School classes. Use this skill whenever the user says \"write a class script\", \"turn this into a class video\", \"script this for the [business/claude-code/prompt-engineering/skills/context-engineering/techniques/claude-chat/claude-cowork/correction] class\", \"draft an agentic coding school video\", or whenever the user is staging content in `/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os/projects/agentic-coding-school/to-film/` and wants it written up. Default to this skill for any new file destined for the `to-film/` tree, even if the user does not name it explicitly. Produces an essay-style markdown script with the correct frontmatter, horizontal rules between sections, inline image embed placeholders, and a Demo section, written in Ray's direct second-person voice.\n- content-cascade\n- discord-archive\n- excalidraw-bundle: Bundle a set of section images into a single .excalidraw file laid out as a grid — one column per section in document order, the section's variations stacked vertically — and save it to ~/Downloads, ready to drag into excalidraw.com or the Obsidian Excalidraw plugin.\n- excalidraw-codex: Generate excalidraw-style explanation images using the Codex CLI (codex exec --json) instead of a paid API. Use when the user wants visual explanations, diagrams, or illustrations in the hand-drawn excalidraw aesthetic and asks for the codex path. Codex's bundled image_gen tool runs through the logged-in account, so no GEMINI_API_KEY is required and it's often faster than the Gemini route. This is the RECOMMENDED DEFAULT path for excalidraw image generation: it needs no API key and runs through the logged-in Codex account. Default to codex whenever the user does not explicitly ask for the Gemini route.\n- excalidraw-codex-simple\n- excalidraw-deck: Build hand-drawn, Excalidraw-style HTML slide decks that explain a concept through sketch-aesthetic vector diagrams — rough.js strokes on a dark canvas, full-bleed, one diagram per slide with a caption underneath, arrow-key and tap navigation. Use this skill whenever the user wants explainer slides, a concept deck, diagram slides, or a talk/video presentation in the hand-drawn sketch look, or says things like \"make slides for X\", \"build a deck for X\", \"add a slide in this style\", \"turn this into a sketch/excalidraw-style deck\", \"another slide like the workflow deck\", or points at an existing rough.js deck and wants more in the same style. This is for self-contained vector-diagram decks — NOT the Claude Code terminal-aesthetic slides (use cc-terminal-slides) and NOT raster image generation (use excalidraw-gen, which calls an image model). Reach for this skill proactively for any \"explain this concept on slides\" request, even when the user does not say the word \"excalidraw\".\n- extract-wisdom: Extract structured wisdom, insights, and actionable takeaways from any content — podcasts, articles, videos, transcripts, books, or conversations. Use this skill whenever the user asks to \"extract wisdom\", \"summarize insights\", \"pull out key ideas\", \"what are the takeaways\", \"extract the best parts\", \"distill this content\", \"what did I learn from this\", or provides a transcript/article and asks for a structured breakdown. Also trigger when the user pastes or references long-form content and wants the signal extracted from the noise — even if they don't use the word \"wisdom\" specifically. Inspired by Daniel Miessler's Fabric pattern. Has a BATCH MODE for running this extraction across a whole YouTube channel in bulk: lists videos with yt-dlp, filters by duration, downloads transcripts, then fans out one extract-wisdom subagent per video in batches of 5, writing one wisdom file per video to disk. Trigger batch mode whenever the user wants wisdom/insights/takeaways from a YouTube channel in bulk: \"extract wisdom from this channel\", \"go through @<handle>'s long videos\", \"summarise everything on <channel>\", \"give me the best ideas from <creator>'s podcast\", \"process a whole channel into wisdom notes\", or \"I want to read the highlights from every video on @<handle> longer than 20 minutes\".\n- humanizer\n- idea-foundry\n- image-cleanup: Clean up unreferenced images in Obsidian vault folders. Deletes unused images, renames excalidraw files with descriptive names based on image content (using vision), and moves everything to vault root images/ folder. Use after picking favorites from excalidraw-gen batches. Triggers on \"clean up images\", \"delete unused images\", \"tidy up images\", \"image cleanup\", or \"/image-cleanup\".\n- linkedin: All LinkedIn tasks — writing posts (text or carousel), scouting sources to post about, checking post performance, browsing the feed, or analyzing competitors. Triggers on requests like \"write a newsletter\", \"draft a newsletter about...\", \"help me write my weekly email\", \"newsletter style\", or when the user provides a topic and wants it turned into a newsletter format.\n- rakuten-hotels\n- recut-xml-fixer: Reshape an FCP7 XML exported from a silence-cutting app (Recut, AutoEdit, Vika, etc.) so Adobe Premiere Pro imports it cleanly with the user's pristine external audio (e.g. Audio.wav from Audacity) linked to the video instead of the camera/screen-recording's own audio. Use this skill whenever the user points at an .xml or sequence file from Recut, AutoEdit, or any auto-cutter and wants to swap in their own audio track, drop the redundant on-camera audio, fix vertical alignment between video and audio cuts, or fix a Premiere \"import failed\" error on a Recut-style XML. Triggers on phrases like \"fix this Recut XML\", \"swap the audio in this premiere XML\", \"align the cuts to my Audio.wav\", \"remove the loom audio track\", \"merge V1 and A1 in this XML so they're linked\", or any reference to a workflow combining a silence-cutter app's XML with a separately recorded pristine audio file.\n- sentence-mining: Build and maintain Japanese sentence-mining cards for Anki, fully self-contained (no AnkiMorphs install required) and configurable per user via a one-time `/sentence-mining setup`. Six modes, and the skill ASKS which one you want on every launch. (1) Video mode — paste any Instagram reel, YouTube video/Short, TikTok, Twitter video, or local file → yt-dlp + AssemblyAI + SudachiPy + a built-in i+1 known-word diff produces draft cards. (2) Bank mode — give a list of target words → find the best example sentence for each through one canonical cascade (Immersion Kit → Nadeshiko → sentencesearch → kotu → your locally-indexed subs2srs .apkg banks), re-ranked by your own i+1, with native audio and a screenshot where the source ships one. (3) Replace mode — fix existing cards whose example sentence is bad (too short, a fragment, incomprehensible): pull a better sentence through the same cascade, edit the card in place (archiving the old sentence to a previous_versions field), and rehabilitate the card so you re-learn it fresh. (4) Leech mode — work the cards Anki flagged as leeches, but filtered by whether they are STILL failing: `tag:leech` is a scar Anki never removes, so most of a leech list is usually cards that recovered months ago (43 of Ray's 78 sat at 21-338 day intervals). Cards below the interval threshold get a fresh sentence through the cascade plus full rehabilitation (reps/lapses zeroed, due today); recovered cards just have the tag stripped with their scheduling untouched; words rarer than a JPDB frequen…\n- transcribe-youtube-video: Transcribe audio or video into a clean .txt + timestamped .srt + YouTube chapter timestamps. Re-encodes any WAV/MP4 to a small MP3 before upload, mines on-screen text with OCR to seed AssemblyAI key terms, runs AssemblyAI Universal-3 Pro speech-to-text, then generates punchy chapter markers from the SRT. Use whenever the user wants to transcribe a video or audio file, generate subtitles/captions or an SRT, create a YouTube transcript or chapters, or convert a recording to text. Also trigger when the user has a WAV/MP4/MOV and wants text out of it, even without saying 'transcribe'.\n- ungate-claude-code-feature: Enable a gated, experimental, or staged-rollout Claude Code feature on your own machine by finding its GrowthBook feature flag in the binary and force-enabling it through a Proxyman interception rule (with an optional disk-cache fast-path). Use this skill whenever the user wants to ungate, unlock, force-enable, or \"turn on\" a Claude Code feature hidden behind a flag — phrasings like \"ungate the X feature\", \"enable ultrareview\", \"unlock [feature name]\", \"turn on [feature]\", \"enable the workflow/agent tool\", \"this feature won't show up even with the env var\", \"force-enable a tengu_ flag\", \"flip the flag for X\", \"intercept the feature flag\", \"unlock a beta Claude Code feature\", or any case where a Claude Code feature is gated and the user wants it on. Also trigger when the user references a specific tengu_ flag name, or the existing \"GrowthBook Flag Enabler\" Proxyman rule, or wants to add another flag to it. For the user's own Claude Code install and account.\n- watch-next-jp\n- wisdom-to-acs-gap: Turn ONE video (YouTube URL/id or a pasted transcript) into an Agentic Coding School (ACS) content-gap report: extract its full wisdom, promote the 1-3 load-bearing \"spine\" ideas, deep-dive each in prose, gap-check each against the live ACS catalog, and pitch the buildable new ACS videos. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- claude-md-management:claude-md-improver\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Outrank batches\n34\t// are small; a few MB is generous headroom for a legitimate publish.\n35\tconst MAX_BODY_BYTES = 5 * 1024 * 1024; // 5 MB\n36\t// Cap the number of articles processed in a single delivery so one request\n37\t// cannot tie up the function for an unbounded number of serial DB round-trips.\n38\tconst MAX_ARTICLES = 100;\n39\t\n40\ttype OutrankArticle = {\n41\t  id?: string;\n42\t  title?: string;\n43\t  content_markdown?: string;\n44\t  content_html?: string;\n45\t  meta_description?: string;\n46\t  description?: string;\n47\t  created_at?: string;\n48\t  published_at?: string;\n49\t  image_url?: string;\n50\t  image_alt?: string;\n51\t  slug?: string;\n52\t  tags?: string[];\n53\t  url?: string;\n54\t};\n55\t\n56\ttype OutrankPayload = {\n57\t  event_type?: string;\n58\t  timestamp?: string;\n59\t  data?: { articles?: OutrankArticle[] };\n60\t};\n61\t\n62\t/**\n63\t * Verify a per-request HMAC-SHA256 signature over the raw body. Returns false\n64\t * when no signing secret is configured (so the caller can fall back to the\n65\t * static bearer token).\n66\t */\n67\tfunction isSignatureValid(rawBody: string, header: string | null): boolean {\n68\t  const secret = env.OUTRANK_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET;\n69\t  if (!secret || !header) return false;\n70\t\n71\t  // Accept either a bare hex digest or a `sha256=<hex>` prefixed value.\n72\t  const provided = header.startsWith(\"sha256=\")\n73\t    ? header.slice(\"sha256=\".length).trim()\n74\t    : header.trim();\n75\t\n76\t  const expected = createHmac(\"sha256\", secret).update(rawBody).digest(\"hex\");\n77\t\n78\t  const encoder = new TextEncoder();\n79\t  const providedBuf = encoder.encode(provided.toLowerCase());\n80\t  const expectedBuf = encoder.encode(expected);\n81\t  if (providedBuf.length !== expectedBuf.length) return false;\n82\t  return timingSafeEqual(providedBuf, expectedBuf);\n83\t}\n84\t\n85\tfunction isBearerAuthorized(req: NextRequest): boolean {\n86\t  const expected = env.OUTRANK_WEBHOOK_TOKEN;\n87\t  if (!expected) return false;\n88\t  const header = req.headers.get(\"authorization\");\n89\t  if (!header || !header.startsWith(\"Bearer \")) return false;\n90\t  return timingSafeEqualSecret(header.slice(\"Bearer \".length).trim(), expected);\n91\t}\n92\t\n93\tfunction slugify(input: string): string {\n94\t  return input\n95\t    .toString()\n96\t    .toLowerCase()\n97\t    .normalize(\"NFKD\")\n98\t    .replace(/[̀-ͯ]/g, \"\")\n99\t    .replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, \"-\")\n100\t    .replace(/^-+|-+$/g, \"\")\n101\t    .slice(0, 120);\n102\t}\n103\t\n104\tconst LOCALE = \"en\";\n105\t\n106\t/**\n107\t * Return true when `slug` is free within the locale, i.e. not already claimed\n108\t * by a DIFFERENT external article. (A row owned by `externalId` itself is fine\n109\t * — the upsert resolves that on the externalId conflict target.)\n110\t */\n111\tasync function isSlugFree(slug: string, externalId: string): Promise<boolean> {\n112\t  const existing = await db\n113\t    .select({ id: blogPosts.id })\n114\t    .from(blogPosts)\n115\t    .where(\n116\t      and(\n117\t        eq(blogPosts.locale, LOCALE),\n118\t        eq(blogPosts.slug, slug),\n119\t        ne(blogPosts.externalId, externalId),\n120\t      ),\n121\t    )\n122\t    .limit(1);\n123\t  return existing.length === 0;\n124\t}\n125\t\n126\t/**\n127\t * Resolve a slug that is unique within the locale. If the desired slug is\n128\t * already taken by a DIFFERENT external article, append a suffix derived from\n129\t * the external id so the new article still persists instead of throwing a\n130\t * (locale, slug) unique violation. The first candidate is verified too — and if\n131\t * it is also taken (e.g. duplicate-title articles whose external ids share the\n132\t * leading 8 alphanumerics) we widen the suffix and finally fall back to the\n133\t * full external id, which is globally unique, so a free slug is guaranteed.\n134\t */\n135\tasync function resolveUniqueSlug(\n136\t  desired: string,\n137\t  externalId: string,\n138\t): Promise<string> {\n139\t  if (await isSlugFree(desired, externalId)) return desired;\n140\t\n141\t  const sanitizedId = externalId.replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/gi, \"\").toLowerCase();\n142\t\n143\t  // Try progressively longer slices of the external id, then the full id, so\n144\t  // articles that collide on a short shared prefix still resolve to distinct\n145\t  // slugs. Each candidate is verified before use.\n146\t  const suffixLengths = [8, 16, sanitizedId.length];\n147\t  for (const len of suffixLengths) {\n148\t    const suffix = sanitizedId.slice(0, len);\n149\t    if (!suffix) continue;\n150\t    const base = desired.slice(0, 120 - (suffix.length + 1));\n151\t    const candidate = `${base}-${suffix}`;\n152\t    if (await isSlugFree(candidate, externalId)) return candidate;\n153\t  }\n154\t\n155\t  // Last resort: a timestamp keeps the slug unique even when the external id\n156\t  // alone somehow still collides, so the article persists rather than being\n157\t  // dropped on a unique violation.\n158\t  const suffix = `${sanitizedId || \"post\"}-${Date.now().toString(36)}`;\n159\t  const base = desired.slice(0, 120 - (suffix.length + 1));\n160\t  return `${base}-${suffix}`;\n161\t}\n162\t\n163\tfunction escapeHtml(value: string): string {\n164\t  return value\n165\t    .replace(/&/g, \"&amp;\")\n166\t    .replace(/</g, \"&lt;\")\n167\t    .replace(/>/g, \"&gt;\")\n168\t    .replace(/\"/g, \"&quot;\")\n169\t    .replace(/'/g, \"&#39;\");\n170\t}\n171\t\n172\tfunction markdownToHtml(markdown: string): string {\n173\t  return markdown\n174\t    .trim()\n175\t    .split(/\\n{2,}/)\n176\t    .map((block) => {\n177\t      const text = escapeHtml(block.trim());\n178\t      if (!text) return \"\";\n179\t\n180\t      const heading = text.match(/^(#{1,3})\\s+(.+)$/);\n181\t      if (heading) {\n182\t        const level = heading[1].length;\n183\t        return `<h${level}>${heading[2]}</h${level}>`;\n184\t      }\n185\t\n186\t      return `<p>${text.replace(/\\n/g, \"<br />\")}</p>`;\n187\t    })\n188\t    .filter(Boolean)\n189\t    .join(\"\\n\");\n190\t}\n191\t\n192\tfunction parsePublishedAt(article: OutrankArticle): {\n193\t  publishedAt?: Date;\n194\t  error?: string;\n195\t} {\n196\t  const candidate = article.published_at ?? article.created_at;\n197\t  if (!candidate) return {};\n198\t\n199\t  const parsed = new Date(candidate);\n200\t  if (Number.isNaN(parsed.getTime())) {\n201\t    const field = article.published_at ? \"published_at\" : \"created_at\";\n202\t    return { error: `invalid ${field}: ${candidate}` };\n203\t  }\n204\t\n205\t  return { publishedAt: parsed };\n206\t}\n207\t\n208\tfunction normalizeTags(tags: unknown): string[] {\n209\t  if (!Array.isArray(tags)) return [];\n210\t\n211\t  return tags\n212\t    .filter((tag): tag is string => typeof tag === \"string\")\n213\t    .map((tag) => tag.trim())\n214\t    .filter(Boolean)\n215\t    .slice(0, 12);\n216\t}\n217\t\n218\texport async function POST(req: NextRequest) {\n219\t  const contentLength = Number(req.headers.get(\"content-length\"));\n220\t  if (Number.isFinite(contentLength) && contentLength > MAX_BODY_BYTES) {\n221\t    return NextResponse.json({ error: \"Payload too large\" }, { status: 413 });\n222\t  }\n223\t\n224\t  // Signature verification needs the raw bytes, so we can only HMAC-check after\n225\t  // buffering the body. The bearer token, by contrast, is independent of the\n226\t  // payload — so when HMAC signing is disabled (the normal direct-Outrank\n227\t  // setup) reject a bad bearer token BEFORE allocating the body, instead of\n228\t  // buffering an unsigned, unauthenticated request.\n229\t  const signature =\n230\t    req.headers.get(\"x-outrank-signature\") ??\n231\t    req.headers.get(\"outrank-signature\");\n232\t  const signingEnabled = Boolean(env.OUTRANK_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET);\n233\t  if (!signingEnabled && !isBearerAuthorized(req)) {\n234\t    return NextResponse.json(\n235\t      { error: \"Invalid access token\" },\n236\t      { status: 401 },\n237\t    );\n238\t  }\n239\t\n240\t  // Read the raw body once so we can both size-check it and verify an HMAC\n241\t  // signature over the exact bytes.\n242\t  let rawBody: string;\n243\t  try {\n244\t    rawBody = await req.text();\n245\t  } catch {\n246\t    return NextResponse.json({ error: \"Invalid body\" }, { status: 400 });\n247\t  }\n248\t\n249\t  if (Buffer.byteLength(rawBody, \"utf8\") > MAX_BODY_BYTES) {\n250\t    return NextResponse.json({ error: \"Payload too large\" }, { status: 413 });\n251\t  }\n252\t\n253\t  // When signing is enabled, prefer HMAC signature verification but still fall\n254\t  // back to the static bearer token for callers that have not migrated to\n255\t  // signing. (When signing is disabled the bearer token was already verified\n256\t  // above before the body was buffered.)\n257\t  if (signingEnabled) {\n258\t    const authorized =\n259\t      isSignatureValid(rawBody, signature) || isBearerAuthorized(req);\n260\t    if (!authorized) {\n261\t      return NextResponse.json(\n262\t        { error: \"Invalid access token\" },\n263\t        { status: 401 },\n264\t      );\n265\t    }\n266\t  }\n267\t\n268\t  let payload: OutrankPayload;\n269\t  try {\n270\t    payload = JSON.parse(rawBody) as OutrankPayload;\n271\t  } catch {\n272\t    return NextResponse.json({ error: \"Invalid JSON\" }, { status: 400 });\n273\t  }\n274\t\n275\t  if (payload.event_type !== \"publish_articles\") {\n276\t    return NextResponse.json(\n277\t      { error: `Unsupported event_type: ${payload.event_type}` },\n278\t      { status: 400 },\n279\t    );\n280\t  }\n281\t\n282\t  const articles = payload.data?.articles;\n283\t  if (!Array.isArray(articles) || articles.length === 0) {\n284\t    return NextResponse.json(\n285\t      { error: \"No articles in payload\" },\n286\t      { status: 400 },\n287\t    );\n288\t  }\n289\t\n290\t  if (articles.length > MAX_ARTICLES) {\n291\t    return NextResponse.json(\n292\t      {\n293\t        error: `Too many articles: ${articles.length} (max ${MAX_ARTICLES})`,\n294\t      },\n295\t      { status: 413 },\n296\t    );\n297\t  }\n298\t\n299\t  const processed: Array<{ id: string; slug: string }> = [];\n300\t  const skipped: Array<{ index: number; reason: string }> = [];\n301\t  // Track whether any skip was a TRANSIENT failure (e.g. a DB upsert error)\n302\t  // rather than a permanent validation error. Only transient failures justify\n303\t  // a non-2xx that asks the sender to retry; a payload whose articles are all\n304\t  // permanently invalid must not be retried forever.\n305\t  let hadTransientFailure = false;\n306\t\n307\t  for (let i = 0; i < articles.length; i++) {\n308\t    const article = articles[i];\n309\t    const markdown = article?.content_markdown?.trim() ?? \"\";\n310\t    const html = article?.content_html?.trim() || markdownToHtml(markdown);\n311\t    const description = article?.meta_description ?? article?.description;\n312\t\n313\t    if (!article?.id || !article.title?.trim() || (!markdown && !html)) {\n314\t      skipped.push({\n315\t        index: i,\n316\t        reason: \"missing id/title/content\",\n317\t      });\n318\t      continue;\n319\t    }\n320\t\n321\t    const title = article.title.trim();\n322\t    const slugSource = article.slug?.trim() || title;\n323\t    const baseSlug = slugify(slugSource);\n324\t    if (!baseSlug) {\n325\t      skipped.push({ index: i, reason: \"slug resolved to empty string\" });\n326\t      continue;\n327\t    }\n328\t\n329\t    // Validate published_at/created_at before either reaches the driver: an Invalid Date\n330\t    // makes node-postgres throw RangeError on serialization, which would\n331\t    // otherwise be swallowed as a silent skip.\n332\t    const { publishedAt, error: publishedAtError } = parsePublishedAt(article);\n333\t    if (publishedAtError) {\n334\t      skipped.push({\n335\t        index: i,\n336\t        reason: publishedAtError,\n337\t      });\n338\t      continue;\n339\t    }\n340\t\n341\t    const tags = normalizeTags(article.tags);\n342\t\n343\t    try {\n344\t      // Avoid a (locale, slug) unique violation when a different article has\n345\t      // already claimed this slug.\n346\t      const slug = await resolveUniqueSlug(baseSlug, article.id);\n347\t\n348\t      const [row] = await db\n349\t        .insert(blogPosts)\n350\t        .values({\n351\t          externalId: article.id,\n352\t          source: \"outrank\",\n353\t          locale: LOCALE,\n354\t          slug,\n355\t          title,\n356\t          description: description?.trim() || null,\n357\t          contentMarkdown: markdown,\n358\t          contentHtml: html,\n359\t          imageUrl: article.image_url ?? null,\n360\t          imageAlt: article.image_alt ?? title,\n361\t          tags,\n362\t          // Falls back to the column default (now()) on first insert.\n363\t          ...(publishedAt ? { publishedAt } : {}),\n364\t        })\n365\t        .onConflictDoUpdate({\n366\t          target: blogPosts.externalId,\n367\t          set: {\n368\t            slug,\n369\t            title,\n370\t            description: description?.trim() || null,\n371\t            contentMarkdown: markdown,\n372\t            contentHtml: html,\n373\t            imageUrl: article.image_url ?? null,\n374\t            imageAlt: article.image_alt ?? title,\n375\t            tags,\n376\t            // Only overwrite publishedAt when the payload supplied a date, so\n377\t            // a re-publish without created_at does not reset ordering state.\n378\t            ...(publishedAt ? { publishedAt } : {}),\n379\t            updatedAt: new Date(),\n380\t          },\n381\t        })\n382\t        .returning({ id: blogPosts.id, slug: blogPosts.slug });\n383\t\n384\t      processed.push({ id: row.id, slug: row.slug });\n385\t\n386\t      revalidatePath(`/en/blog/${row.slug}`);\n387\t    } catch (err) {\n388\t      console.error(\"[add-blog-post] upsert failed\", {\n389\t        externalId: article.id,\n390\t        err,\n391\t      });\n392\t      // A thrown upsert is treated as transient (DB outage, constraint, etc.)\n393\t      // so the delivery is retryable.\n394\t      hadTransientFailure = true;\n395\t      skipped.push({\n396\t        index: i,\n397\t        reason: err instanceof Error ? err.message : \"upsert failed\",\n398\t      });\n399\t    }\n400\t  }\n401\t\n402\t  if (processed.length > 0) {\n403\t    revalidatePath(\"/en/blog\");\n404\t    revalidatePath(\"/sitemap.xml\");\n405\t  }\n406\t\n407\t  // If nothing was persisted because of a TRANSIENT failure (a DB upsert\n408\t  // threw), return a non-2xx so the sender retries instead of treating the\n409\t  // silent failure as success. Validation-only skips are permanent client/data\n410\t  // errors — retrying them never succeeds — so those return 2xx below.\n411\t  if (processed.length === 0 && hadTransientFailure) {\n412\t    return NextResponse.json(\n413\t      {\n414\t        message: \"Webhook failed: no articles were persisted\",\n415\t        processed: 0,\n416\t        skipped: skipped.length,\n417\t        skippedDetails: skipped,\n418\t      },\n419\t      { status: 500 },\n420\t    );\n421\t  }\n422\t\n423\t  return NextResponse.json({\n424\t    message: \"Webhook processed successfully\",\n425\t    processed: processed.length,\n426\t    skipped: skipped.length,\n427\t    articles: processed,\n428\t    ...(skipped.length > 0 ? { skippedDetails: skipped } : {}),\n429\t  });\n430\t}\n431\t"}]},"uuid":"7fc738c7-332d-46fe-bcb6-6685b770151b","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:40:46.796Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/app/api/webhooks/add-blog-post/route.ts","content":"import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from \"next/server\";\nimport { revalidatePath } from \"next/cache\";\nimport { createHmac, timingSafeEqual } from \"node:crypto\";\nimport { and, eq, ne } from \"drizzle-orm\";\nimport { timingSafeEqualSecret } from \"@/lib/security/timing-safe-secret\";\nimport { env } from \"@/src/env/server.mjs\";\nimport { db } from \"@/src/db\";\nimport { blogPosts } from \"@/src/db/schema/blog-posts\";\n\n/**\n * Outrank \"publish_articles\" webhook.\n *\n * Auth (issue #842): Outrank itself can only authenticate with a static\n * `Authorization: Bearer <OUTRANK_WEBHOOK_TOKEN>` — its webhook spec has no\n * request signing, so it never sends a signature header. The bearer token is\n * therefore the operative, permanent auth and must be treated as a long-lived\n * shared secret: keep it secret and rotate it if it may have leaked.\n *\n * The optional HMAC path (OUTRANK_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET) only engages when a\n * signing-capable proxy sits in front of this endpoint and sends a signature\n * header. When that secret is unset (the expected state for direct Outrank\n * deliveries) the bearer token is the sole auth — the HMAC branch is then\n * intentionally inert, not dead code, and exists so an integrity-binding proxy\n * can be added later without a code change.\n *\n * Upserts each article into blog_posts (keyed on external_id) and revalidates\n * the blog routes so new posts appear immediately.\n */\n\nexport const runtime = \"nodejs\";\nexport const dynamic = \"force-dynamic\";\n\n// Reject oversized payloads before buffering them into memory. Outrank batches\n// are small; a few MB is generous headroom for a legitimate publish.\nconst MAX_BODY_BYTES = 5 * 1024 * 1024; // 5 MB\n// Cap the number of articles processed in a single delivery so one request\n// cannot tie up the function for an unbounded number of serial DB round-trips.\nconst MAX_ARTICLES = 100;\n\ntype OutrankArticle = {\n  id?: string;\n  title?: string;\n  content_markdown?: string;\n  content_html?: string;\n  meta_description?: string;\n  description?: string;\n  created_at?: string;\n  published_at?: string;\n  image_url?: string;\n  image_alt?: string;\n  slug?: string;\n  tags?: string[];\n  url?: string;\n};\n\ntype OutrankPayload = {\n  event_type?: string;\n  timestamp?: string;\n  data?: { articles?: OutrankArticle[] };\n};\n\n/**\n * Verify a per-request HMAC-SHA256 signature over the raw body. Returns false\n * when no signing secret is configured (so the caller can fall back to the\n * static bearer token).\n */\nfunction isSignatureValid(rawBody: string, header: string | null): boolean {\n  const secret = env.OUTRANK_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET;\n  if (!secret || !header) return false;\n\n  // Accept either a bare hex digest or a `sha256=<hex>` prefixed value.\n  const provided = header.startsWith(\"sha256=\")\n    ? header.slice(\"sha256=\".length).trim()\n    : header.trim();\n\n  const expected = createHmac(\"sha256\", secret).update(rawBody).digest(\"hex\");\n\n  const encoder = new TextEncoder();\n  const providedBuf = encoder.encode(provided.toLowerCase());\n  const expectedBuf = encoder.encode(expected);\n  if (providedBuf.length !== expectedBuf.length) return false;\n  return timingSafeEqual(providedBuf, expectedBuf);\n}\n\nfunction isBearerAuthorized(req: NextRequest): boolean {\n  const expected = env.OUTRANK_WEBHOOK_TOKEN;\n  if (!expected) return false;\n  const header = req.headers.get(\"authorization\");\n  if (!header || !header.startsWith(\"Bearer \")) return false;\n  return timingSafeEqualSecret(header.slice(\"Bearer \".length).trim(), expected);\n}\n\nfunction slugify(input: string): string {\n  return input\n    .toString()\n    .toLowerCase()\n    .normalize(\"NFKD\")\n    .replace(/[̀-ͯ]/g, \"\")\n    .replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, \"-\")\n    .replace(/^-+|-+$/g, \"\")\n    .slice(0, 120);\n}\n\nconst LOCALE = \"en\";\n\n/**\n * Return true when `slug` is free within the locale, i.e. not already claimed\n * by a DIFFERENT external article. (A row owned by `externalId` itself is fine\n * — the upsert resolves that on the externalId conflict target.)\n */\nasync function isSlugFree(slug: string, externalId: string): Promise<boolean> {\n  const existing = await db\n    .select({ id: blogPosts.id })\n    .from(blogPosts)\n    .where(\n      and(\n        eq(blogPosts.locale, LOCALE),\n        eq(blogPosts.slug, slug),\n        ne(blogPosts.externalId, externalId),\n      ),\n    )\n    .limit(1);\n  return existing.length === 0;\n}\n\n/**\n * Resolve a slug that is unique within the locale. If the desired slug is\n * already taken by a DIFFERENT external article, append a suffix derived from\n * the external id so the new article still persists instead of throwing a\n * (locale, slug) unique violation. The first candidate is verified too — and if\n * it is also taken (e.g. duplicate-title articles whose external ids share the\n * leading 8 alphanumerics) we widen the suffix and finally fall back to the\n * full external id, which is globally unique, so a free slug is guaranteed.\n */\nasync function resolveUniqueSlug(\n  desired: string,\n  externalId: string,\n): Promise<string> {\n  if (await isSlugFree(desired, externalId)) return desired;\n\n  const sanitizedId = externalId.replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/gi, \"\").toLowerCase();\n\n  // Try progressively longer slices of the external id, then the full id, so\n  // articles that collide on a short shared prefix still resolve to distinct\n  // slugs. Each candidate is verified before use.\n  const suffixLengths = [8, 16, sanitizedId.length];\n  for (const len of suffixLengths) {\n    const suffix = sanitizedId.slice(0, len);\n    if (!suffix) continue;\n    const base = desired.slice(0, 120 - (suffix.length + 1));\n    const candidate = `${base}-${suffix}`;\n    if (await isSlugFree(candidate, externalId)) return candidate;\n  }\n\n  // Last resort: a timestamp keeps the slug unique even when the external id\n  // alone somehow still collides, so the article persists rather than being\n  // dropped on a unique violation.\n  const suffix = `${sanitizedId || \"post\"}-${Date.now().toString(36)}`;\n  const base = desired.slice(0, 120 - (suffix.length + 1));\n  return `${base}-${suffix}`;\n}\n\nfunction escapeHtml(value: string): string {\n  return value\n    .replace(/&/g, \"&amp;\")\n    .replace(/</g, \"&lt;\")\n    .replace(/>/g, \"&gt;\")\n    .replace(/\"/g, \"&quot;\")\n    .replace(/'/g, \"&#39;\");\n}\n\nfunction markdownToHtml(markdown: string): string {\n  return markdown\n    .trim()\n    .split(/\\n{2,}/)\n    .map((block) => {\n      const text = escapeHtml(block.trim());\n      if (!text) return \"\";\n\n      const heading = text.match(/^(#{1,3})\\s+(.+)$/);\n      if (heading) {\n        const level = heading[1].length;\n        return `<h${level}>${heading[2]}</h${level}>`;\n      }\n\n      return `<p>${text.replace(/\\n/g, \"<br />\")}</p>`;\n    })\n    .filter(Boolean)\n    .join(\"\\n\");\n}\n\nfunction parsePublishedAt(article: OutrankArticle): {\n  publishedAt?: Date;\n  error?: string;\n} {\n  const candidate = article.published_at ?? article.created_at;\n  if (!candidate) return {};\n\n  const parsed = new Date(candidate);\n  if (Number.isNaN(parsed.getTime())) {\n    const field = article.published_at ? \"published_at\" : \"created_at\";\n    return { error: `invalid ${field}: ${candidate}` };\n  }\n\n  return { publishedAt: parsed };\n}\n\nfunction normalizeTags(tags: unknown): string[] {\n  if (!Array.isArray(tags)) return [];\n\n  return tags\n    .filter((tag): tag is string => typeof tag === \"string\")\n    .map((tag) => tag.trim())\n    .filter(Boolean)\n    .slice(0, 12);\n}\n\nexport async function POST(req: NextRequest) {\n  const contentLength = Number(req.headers.get(\"content-length\"));\n  if (Number.isFinite(contentLength) && contentLength > MAX_BODY_BYTES) {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: \"Payload too large\" }, { status: 413 });\n  }\n\n  // Signature verification needs the raw bytes, so we can only HMAC-check after\n  // buffering the body. The bearer token, by contrast, is independent of the\n  // payload — so when HMAC signing is disabled (the normal direct-Outrank\n  // setup) reject a bad bearer token BEFORE allocating the body, instead of\n  // buffering an unsigned, unauthenticated request.\n  const signature =\n    req.headers.get(\"x-outrank-signature\") ??\n    req.headers.get(\"outrank-signature\");\n  const signingEnabled = Boolean(env.OUTRANK_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET);\n  if (!signingEnabled && !isBearerAuthorized(req)) {\n    return NextResponse.json(\n      { error: \"Invalid access token\" },\n      { status: 401 },\n    );\n  }\n\n  // Read the raw body once so we can both size-check it and verify an HMAC\n  // signature over the exact bytes.\n  let rawBody: string;\n  try {\n    rawBody = await req.text();\n  } catch {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: \"Invalid body\" }, { status: 400 });\n  }\n\n  if (Buffer.byteLength(rawBody, \"utf8\") > MAX_BODY_BYTES) {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: \"Payload too large\" }, { status: 413 });\n  }\n\n  // When signing is enabled, prefer HMAC signature verification but still fall\n  // back to the static bearer token for callers that have not migrated to\n  // signing. (When signing is disabled the bearer token was already verified\n  // above before the body was buffered.)\n  if (signingEnabled) {\n    const authorized =\n      isSignatureValid(rawBody, signature) || isBearerAuthorized(req);\n    if (!authorized) {\n      return NextResponse.json(\n        { error: \"Invalid access token\" },\n        { status: 401 },\n      );\n    }\n  }\n\n  let payload: OutrankPayload;\n  try {\n    payload = JSON.parse(rawBody) as OutrankPayload;\n  } catch {\n    return NextResponse.json({ error: \"Invalid JSON\" }, { status: 400 });\n  }\n\n  if (payload.event_type !== \"publish_articles\") {\n    return NextResponse.json(\n      { error: `Unsupported event_type: ${payload.event_type}` },\n      { status: 400 },\n    );\n  }\n\n  const articles = payload.data?.articles;\n  if (!Array.isArray(articles) || articles.length === 0) {\n    return NextResponse.json(\n      { error: \"No articles in payload\" },\n      { status: 400 },\n    );\n  }\n\n  if (articles.length > MAX_ARTICLES) {\n    return NextResponse.json(\n      {\n        error: `Too many articles: ${articles.length} (max ${MAX_ARTICLES})`,\n      },\n      { status: 413 },\n    );\n  }\n\n  const processed: Array<{ id: string; slug: string }> = [];\n  const skipped: Array<{ index: number; reason: string }> = [];\n  // Track whether any skip was a TRANSIENT failure (e.g. a DB upsert error)\n  // rather than a permanent validation error. Only transient failures justify\n  // a non-2xx that asks the sender to retry; a payload whose articles are all\n  // permanently invalid must not be retried forever.\n  let hadTransientFailure = false;\n\n  for (let i = 0; i < articles.length; i++) {\n    const article = articles[i];\n    const markdown = article?.content_markdown?.trim() ?? \"\";\n    const html = article?.content_html?.trim() || markdownToHtml(markdown);\n    const description = article?.meta_description ?? article?.description;\n\n    if (!article?.id || !article.title?.trim() || (!markdown && !html)) {\n      skipped.push({\n        index: i,\n        reason: \"missing id/title/content\",\n      });\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    const title = article.title.trim();\n    const slugSource = article.slug?.trim() || title;\n    const baseSlug = slugify(slugSource);\n    if (!baseSlug) {\n      skipped.push({ index: i, reason: \"slug resolved to empty string\" });\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    // Validate published_at/created_at before either reaches the driver: an Invalid Date\n    // makes node-postgres throw RangeError on serialization, which would\n    // otherwise be swallowed as a silent skip.\n    const { publishedAt, error: publishedAtError } = parsePublishedAt(article);\n    if (publishedAtError) {\n      skipped.push({\n        index: i,\n        reason: publishedAtError,\n      });\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    const tags = normalizeTags(article.tags);\n\n    try {\n      // Avoid a (locale, slug) unique violation when a different article has\n      // already claimed this slug.\n      const slug = await resolveUniqueSlug(baseSlug, article.id);\n\n      const [row] = await db\n        .insert(blogPosts)\n        .values({\n          externalId: article.id,\n          source: \"outrank\",\n          locale: LOCALE,\n          slug,\n          title,\n          description: description?.trim() || null,\n          contentMarkdown: markdown,\n          contentHtml: html,\n          imageUrl: article.image_url ?? null,\n          imageAlt: article.image_alt ?? title,\n          tags,\n          // Falls back to the column default (now()) on first insert.\n          ...(publishedAt ? { publishedAt } : {}),\n        })\n        .onConflictDoUpdate({\n          target: blogPosts.externalId,\n          set: {\n            slug,\n            title,\n            description: description?.trim() || null,\n            contentMarkdown: markdown,\n            contentHtml: html,\n            imageUrl: article.image_url ?? null,\n            imageAlt: article.image_alt ?? title,\n            tags,\n            // Only overwrite publishedAt when the payload supplied a date, so\n            // a re-publish without created_at does not reset ordering state.\n            ...(publishedAt ? { publishedAt } : {}),\n            updatedAt: new Date(),\n          },\n        })\n        .returning({ id: blogPosts.id, slug: blogPosts.slug });\n\n      processed.push({ id: row.id, slug: row.slug });\n\n      revalidatePath(`/en/blog/${row.slug}`);\n    } catch (err) {\n      console.error(\"[add-blog-post] upsert failed\", {\n        externalId: article.id,\n        err,\n      });\n      // A thrown upsert is treated as transient (DB outage, constraint, etc.)\n      // so the delivery is retryable.\n      hadTransientFailure = true;\n      skipped.push({\n        index: i,\n        reason: err instanceof Error ? err.message : \"upsert failed\",\n      });\n    }\n  }\n\n  if (processed.length > 0) {\n    revalidatePath(\"/en/blog\");\n    revalidatePath(\"/sitemap.xml\");\n  }\n\n  // If nothing was persisted because of a TRANSIENT failure (a DB upsert\n  // threw), return a non-2xx so the sender retries instead of treating the\n  // silent failure as success. Validation-only skips are permanent client/data\n  // errors — retrying them never succeeds — so those return 2xx below.\n  if (processed.length === 0 && hadTransientFailure) {\n    return NextResponse.json(\n      {\n        message: \"Webhook failed: no articles were persisted\",\n        processed: 0,\n        skipped: skipped.length,\n        skippedDetails: skipped,\n      },\n      { status: 500 },\n    );\n  }\n\n  return NextResponse.json({\n    message: \"Webhook processed successfully\",\n    processed: processed.length,\n    skipped: skipped.length,\n    articles: processed,\n    ...(skipped.length > 0 ? { skippedDetails: skipped } : {}),\n  });\n}\n","numLines":431,"startLine":1,"totalLines":431}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3ce37bec-c99c-4bfe-a4aa-58ed5d62ffd4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

\"\",\n    date: row.publishedAt.toISOString(),\n    image: row.imageUrl ?? undefined,\n    imageAlt: row.imageAlt ?? undefined,\n    tags: row.tags ?? [],\n    slug: row.slug,\n  };\n\n  return {\n    locale: row.locale,\n    slug: normalizeSlug(row.slug),\n    frontMatter,\n    content: row.contentMarkdown,\n    html: row.contentHtml,\n  };\n}\n\nasync function readAllPosts(): Promise<BlogPost[]> {\n  try {\n    const rows = await db\n      .select()\n      .from(blogPosts)\n      .orderBy(desc(blogPosts.publishedAt));\n    return rows\n      .filter((row) => row.locale === BLOG_LOCALE)\n      .map(rowToPost);\n  } catch (err) {\n    console.error(\"[blog] failed to load posts from DB:\", err);\n    return [];\n  }\n}\n\nexport async function getBlogPosts(): Promise<BlogPostSummary[]> {\n  const posts = await readAllPosts();\n  return posts.map((post) => ({\n    locale: post.locale,\n    slug: post.slug,\n    frontMatter: post.frontMatter,\n  }));\n}\n\nexport async function getBlogPost(slug: string): Promise<BlogPost | null> {\n  const normalizedSlug = normalizeSlug(slug);\n  const posts = await readAllPosts();\n  return posts.find((item) => item.slug === normalizedSlug) ?? null;\n}\n\nexport async function getAllBlogPosts(): Promise<BlogPostSummary[]> {\n  return getBlogPosts();\n}\n","numLines":88,"startLine":1,"totalLines":88}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ff9cf898-5d67-42ac-a319-894520b27be1","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

to-gray-400 bg-clip-text text-4xl font-bold text-transparent md:text-6xl\">\n          Voice AI guides for faster, cleaner writing\n        </h1>\n        <p className=\"mx-auto mt-5 max-w-2xl text-lg text-gray-400\">\n          Practical comparisons, workflow notes, and product updates for people\n          using speech-to-text every day.\n        </p>\n      </header>\n\n      {posts.length === 0 ? (\n        <p className=\"mt-12 text-center text-gray-400\">No posts yet.</p>\n      ) : null}\n\n      {featuredPost ? (\n        <article className=\"mt-12 overflow-hidden rounded-lg border border-gray-800 bg-gray-900/60 shadow-2xl shadow-purple-950/20\">\n          <Link\n            className=\"grid gap-0 md:grid-cols-[1.08fr_0.92fr]\"\n            href={`/blog/${featuredPost.slug}`}\n          >\n            <div className=\"flex min-h-[320px] flex-col justify-between p-7 md:p-9\">\n              <div>\n                <p className=\"text-sm text-gray-500\">\n                  {formatDate(featuredPost.frontMatter.date)}\n                </p>\n                <h2 className=\"mt-4 text-3xl font-semibold leading-tight text-white md:text-4xl\">\n                  {featuredPost.frontMatter.title}\n                </h2>\n                <p className=\"mt-4 text-base leading-7 text-gray-300\">\n                  {featuredPost.frontMatter.description}\n                </p>\n              </div>\n\n              <div className=\"mt-8 flex items-center gap-3 text-sm font-semibold text-purple-300\">\n                Read article\n                <ArrowRight className=\"h-4 w-4\" />\n              </div>\n            </div>\n\n            <div className=\"relative min-h-[260px] border-t border-gray-800 bg-gray-950 md:border-l md:border-t-0\">\n              {featuredPost.frontMatter.image ? Always escape user-derived values before embedding them in HTML.\n9\t */\n10\texport const escapeHtml = (value: string): string =>\n11\t    value\n12\t        .replace(/&/g, \"&amp;\")\n13\t        .replace(/</g, \"&lt;\")\n14\t        .replace(/>/g, \"&gt;\")\n15\t        .replace(/\"/g, \"&quot;\")\n16\t        .replace(/'/g, \"&#39;\");\n17\t"}]},"uuid":"eb1c1e59-4339-492f-949f-88e6ba124204","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:41:23.165Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/lib/templates/escape-html.ts","content":"/**\n * Escape a string for safe interpolation into HTML email markup.\n *\n * Values such as the customer name originate from attacker-controllable\n * sources (e.g. Stripe checkout `customer_details.name`, where a buyer sets\n * their own billing name). eslint: {\n        // Disable ESLint during production builds\n        // ESLint will still run during development (npm run lint)\n        ignoreDuringBuilds: true,\n    },\n    // Webpack configuration\n    webpack: (config) => {\n        // Allow importing .txt files as raw strings (asset/source)\n        config.module.rules.push({\n            test: /\\.txt$/i,\n            type: 'asset/source',\n        });\n        return config;\n    },\n};\n\nexport default withNextIntl(nextConfig);=== MIDDLEWARE matcher ===\nimport createMiddleware from \"next-intl/middleware\";\r\nimport { NextRequest, NextResponse } from \"next/server\";\r\n\r\nimport { routing } from \"./src/i18n/routing\";\r\nimport { defaultLocale, locales } from \"./src/i18n/locales\";\r\nimport { sanitizeReturnTo } from \"./src/lib/license-key-redirect\";\r\n\r\nconst intlMiddleware = createMiddleware(routing);\r\nconst localePattern = locales\r\n  .map((locale) => locale.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\\]\\\\]/g, \"\\\\$&\"))\r\n  .join(\"|\");\r\nconst LOCALE_REGEX = new RegExp(`^\\\\/(${localePattern})(\\\\/|$)`);\r\nconst USER_ROUTE_REGEX = new RegExp(`^\\\\/(${localePattern})\\\\/user`);\r\nconst USER_SIGN_IN_REGEX = new RegExp(`^\\\\/(${localePattern})\\\\/user\\\\/sign-in`);\r\nconst USER_AUTH_SIGN_OUT_REGEX = new RegExp(\r\n  `^\\\\/(${localePattern})\\\\/user\\\\/auth\\\\/sign-out`,\r\n);\r\nconst USER_CUSTOMERS_REGEX = new RegExp(`^\\\\/(${localePattern})\\\\/user\\\\/customers`);\r\n\r\nconst getPathLocale = (pathname: string) => {\r\n  const match = pathname.match(LOCALE_REGEX);\r\n  return match?.[1] ?? defaultLocale;\r\n};\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Check if Better Auth session cookie is present.\r\n * For middleware, we only check cookie presence (no HTTP round-trip).\r\n * Actual session validation happens in the API layer.\r\n *\r\n * Better Auth prefixes cookies with \"__Secure-\" in production when\r\n * baseURL starts with \"https://\", so we check both names.\r\n */\r\nfunction hasSessionCookie(request: NextRequest): boolean {\r\n  return !!(\r\n    request.cookies.get(\"better-auth.session_token\")?.value ||\r\n    request.cookies.get(\"__Secure-better-auth.session_token\")?.value\r\n  );\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Get full Better Auth session via API call.\r\n * Only used for admin routes that need the user's email.\r\n */\r\nasync function getBetterAuthSession(request: NextRequest) {\r\n  const sessionCookie =\r\n    request.cookies.get(\"__Secure-better-auth.session_token\") ||\r\n    request.cookies.get(\"better-auth.session_token\");\r\n  if (!sessionCookie?.value) return null;\r\n\r\n  try {\r\n    const baseUrl = request.nextUrl.origin;\r\n    const response = await fetch(`${baseUrl}/api/auth/get-session`, {\r\n      headers: {\r\n        cookie: request.headers.get(\"cookie\") || \"\",\r\n      },\r\n    });\r\n\r\n    if (!response.ok) return null;\r\n\r\n    const data = await response.json();\r\n    return data?.user ?? null;\r\n  } catch {\r\n    return null;\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Middleware that handles:\r\n * 1. next-intl locale routing (adds locale prefix)\r\n * 2. Better Auth session checking\r\n * 3. User route protection (unified portal for customers and admins)\r\n */\r\nexport default async function middleware(request: NextRequest) {\r\n  const { pathname } = request.nextUrl;\r\n\r\n  // Check if this is a user route (matches /<locale>/user/*)\r\n  const isUserRoute = USER_ROUTE_REGEX.test(pathname);\r\n  const isUserSignIn = USER_SIGN_IN_REGEX.test(pathname);\r\n  const isUserAuthSignOut = USER_AUTH_SIGN_OUT_REGEX.test(pathname);\r\n  const isUserCustomers = USER_CUSTOMERS_REGEX.test(pathname);\r\n\r\n  // =============================================================\r\n  // USER ROUTES - Unified portal for customers and admins\r\n  // =============================================================\r\n\r\n  // For /user/customers, require admin access\r\n  if (isUserCustomers) {\r\n    const user = await getBetterAuthSession(request);\r\n\r\n    // If not authenticated, redirect to user sign-in\r\n    if (!user) {\r\n      const locale = getPathLocale(pathname);\r\n      const signInUrl = new URL(`/${locale}/user/sign-in`, request.url);\r\n      signInUrl.searchParams.set(\"returnTo\", pathname);\r\n      return NextResponse.redirect(signInUrl);\r\n    }\r\n\r\n    // Must be admin to access customers page\r\n    if (user.role !== \"admin\") {\r\n      const locale = getPathLocale(pathname);\r\n      return NextResponse.redirect(\r\n        new URL(`/${locale}/user/dashboard`, request.url)\r\n      );\r\n    }\r\n\r\n    // User is authenticated and is admin - run intl middleware\r\n    const response = intlMiddleware(request) as NextResponse;\r\n    response.headers.set(\"x-pathname\", pathname);\r\n    return response;\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  // For other user routes (except sign-in, sign-out), check authentication via cookie presence\r\n  if (\r\n    isUserRoute &&\r\n    !isUserSignIn &&\r\n    !isUserAuthSignOut\r\n  ) {\r\n    if (!hasSessionCookie(request)) {\r\n      const locale = getPathLocale(pathname);\r\n      const signInUrl = new URL(`/${locale}/user/sign-in`, request.url);","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3bd536f5-f227-4e92-a52e-7faf45b22ac2","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"8562e210-2e64-4fb5-9b94-9ef8a0bcfab4","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"65ea576b-6c0e-4785-96fe-755d0d4890b3","agentId":"a60b3a901f88efa91","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012UYb8z9ZBUhmQxPguggrWu","type":"tool_result","content":"import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from \"next/server\";\n\nimport { auth } from \"@/src/lib/auth\";\nimport {\n  getAccountKeysByEmail,\n  getCreditBalancesForUsers,\n} from \"@/src/lib/db-layer\";\n\n/**\n * Customer Profile API\n *\n * Returns the authenticated customer's profile and license information.\n *\n * GET /api/customer/profile\n * Returns:\n * - user: { id, email }\n * - licenses: Array of license objects with credits\n * - totalCredits: Sum of all credits across licenses\n *\n * Requires authentication via Better Auth session cookie.\n */\nexport async function GET(req: NextRequest) {\n  const session = await auth.api.getSession({ headers: req.headers });\n  if (!session?.user) {\n    return NextResponse.json(\n      { error: \"Unauthorized\" },\n      { status: 401 }\n    );\n  }\n  const user = session.user;\n\n  try {\n    const userEmail = user.email?.toLowerCase() ?? \"\";\n\n    // Fetch user's license keys from database\n    const licenses = await getAccountKeysByEmail(userEmail);\n\n    // Credits are pooled per account. Resolve balances by the licenses' distinct\n    // owning users; each license then reports its account balance.\n    const userIds = Array.from(new Set(licenses.map((l) => l.userId)));\n    const creditMap = await getCreditBalancesForUsers(userIds);\n\n    // Add credits to each license object\n    const licensesWithCredits = licenses.map((license) => ({\n      id: license.id,\n      key: license.key,\n      status: license.status,\n      created_at: license.createdAt.toISOString(),\n      stripe_customer_id: license.stripeCustomerId,\n      polar_customer_id: license.polarCustomerId,\n      credits: creditMap.get(license.userId) || 0,\n    }));\n\n    // Total credits = the sum of the DISTINCT account balances (not a per-license\n    // sum, which would double-count a multi-key account's pooled balance).\n    const totalCredits = userIds.reduce(\n      (sum, uid) => sum + (creditMap.get(uid) || 0),\n      0\n    );","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"723bf558-9983-4625-9d4b-11239b9bd8a2","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:43:30.868Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from \"next/server\";\n\nimport { auth } from \"@/src/lib/auth\";\nimport {\n  getAccountKeysByEmail,\n  getCreditBalancesForUsers,\n} from \"@/src/lib/db-layer\";\n\n/**\n * Customer Profile API\n *\n * Returns the authenticated customer's profile and license information.\n *\n * GET /api/customer/profile\n * Returns:\n * - user: { id, email }\n * - licenses: Array of license objects with credits\n * - totalCredits: Sum of all credits across licenses\n *\n * Requires authentication via Better Auth session cookie.\n */\nexport async function GET(req: NextRequest) {\n  const session = await auth.api.getSession({ headers: req.headers });\n  if (!session?.user) {\n    return NextResponse.json(\n      { error: \"Unauthorized\" },\n      { status: 401 }\n    );\n  }\n  const user = session.user;\n\n  try {\n    const userEmail = user.email?.toLowerCase() ?? \"\";\n\n    // Fetch user's license keys from database\n    const licenses = await getAccountKeysByEmail(userEmail);\n\n    // Credits are pooled per account. Resolve balances by the licenses' distinct\n    // owning users; each license then reports its account balance.\n    const userIds = Array.from(new Set(licenses.map((l) => l.userId)));\n    const creditMap = await getCreditBalancesForUsers(userIds);\n\n    // Add credits to each license object\n    const licensesWithCredits = licenses.map((license) => ({\n      id: license.id,\n      key: license.key,\n      status: license.status,\n      created_at: license.createdAt.toISOString(),\n      stripe_customer_id: license.stripeCustomerId,\n      polar_customer_id: license.polarCustomerId,\n      credits: creditMap.get(license.userId) || 0,\n    }));\n\n    // Total credits = the sum of the DISTINCT account balances (not a per-license\n    // sum, which would double-count a multi-key account's pooled balance).\n    const totalCredits = userIds.reduce(\n      (sum, uid) => sum + (creditMap.get(uid) || 0),\n      0\n    );","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8562e210-2e64-4fb5-9b94-9ef8a0bcfab4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}
